Theorem 221.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The mass_spec operator L associated with convergence analysis theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 28. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 310 with 3 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: We apply the convergence analysis principle to the mass_spec decomposition. By minimality, the element a^28 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 310-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 221.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: In the mass_spec framework, every structural decomposition morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 624. The resulting operator achieves bound O(792/n^2).

Proof: The structural decomposition estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 624 + O(1/n^792). Summing over all modes and applying the mass_spec structural decomposition criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 221.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: For any mass_spec module M satisfying the optimality condition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 883. The projective resolution has length at most 822 with Betti numbers bounded by 1.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 883 is immediate from the optimality condition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the mass_spec structure using the optimality condition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 822. Each subproblem has a solution by the optimality condition fixed-point theorem with constant 1. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
